Replace the whole of `eslint.config.mjs` (an existing file) with:

```js
import { defineConfig, globalIgnores } from "eslint/config";
import nextVitals from "eslint-config-next/core-web-vitals";
import nextTs from "eslint-config-next/typescript";
import prettier from "eslint-config-prettier/flat";

/** Unused identifiers are errors; a leading underscore marks an intentionally unused one. */
const unusedVars = {
  files: ["**/*.{ts,tsx}"],
  rules: {
    "@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars": [
      "error",
      { argsIgnorePattern: "^_", varsIgnorePattern: "^_", caughtErrorsIgnorePattern: "^_" },
    ],
  },
};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Module boundary rules (spec §4, architecture §4).
// PITFALL: in flat config a later object that sets the same rule REPLACES it (no merge).
// So every scope below emits exactly one composed entry per rule, and scopes use disjoint
// files/ignores globs. Never add a second object that sets these rules for overlapping files.
//
// ACCEPTED RESIDUAL RISKS (static linting cannot close these; decided acceptable):
//  - process.env aliasing: `const p = process; p.env`, `globalThis["process"].env`,
//    default/namespace/aliased imports of "node:process" followed by `.env`.
//  - import()/require() with a template or computed specifier (e.g. import(`mongo${"db"}`)).
// Only literal specifiers and the direct `process.env` / `globalThis.process.env` /
// `const { env } = process` / `import { env } from "node:process"` forms are enforced.
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

const MESSAGES = {
  deep: "Import modules only via their public entry '@/modules/<name>'.",
  relIntoModules: "Use '@/modules/<name>', not relative paths into src/modules.",
  escape: "Relative imports must stay inside the module; use '@/modules/<name>'.",
  flatInternal: "internal/ must be flat; no subdirectories.",
  app: "src/modules/** must not import from src/app/**.",
  mongodb: "Only src/modules/database/** may import 'mongodb'.",
  env: "Only src/modules/config/** may read process.env.",
};

/** R1: no deep imports into a module. */
const DEEP_RE = "^@/modules/[^/]+/.+";
const APP_RE = "^@/app(/|$)";
const MONGODB_RE = "^(mongodb|bson)(/|$)";

/**
 * Selectors for dynamic import() and require() with a literal specifier, which the static
 * `patterns` option of no-restricted-imports does not see. esquery regex literals cannot
 * contain "/", so every slash is written as \x2F.
 */
function dynamicSelectors(re, message) {
  const esq = re.replaceAll("/", "\\x2F");
  return [
    { selector: `ImportExpression[source.value=/${esq}/]`, message },
    { selector: `CallExpression[callee.name='require'][arguments.0.value=/${esq}/]`, message },
  ];
}

const R1_DEEP = {
  patterns: [{ regex: DEEP_RE, message: MESSAGES.deep }],
  syntax: dynamicSelectors(DEEP_RE, MESSAGES.deep),
};
/** R1: no relative paths into src/modules from outside. */
const R1_REL_INTO_MODULES = {
  patterns: [{ regex: "^\\.{1,2}/(.+/)?modules/", message: MESSAGES.relIntoModules }],
};
/** R1: files at a module root may not climb out with ../ */
const R1_ESCAPE_1 = { patterns: [{ regex: "^(\\./)*\\.\\./", message: MESSAGES.escape }] };
/** R1: files in module/internal may not climb out with ../../ */
const R1_ESCAPE_2 = {
  patterns: [{ regex: "^(\\./)*\\.\\./(\\./)*\\.\\./", message: MESSAGES.escape }],
};
/** R1: internal/ is flat. Any file in a subdirectory is an error. */
const R1_FLAT_INTERNAL = { syntax: [{ selector: "Program", message: MESSAGES.flatInternal }] };
/** R2: modules never import the app layer. */
const R2_APP = {
  patterns: [{ regex: APP_RE, message: MESSAGES.app }],
  syntax: dynamicSelectors(APP_RE, MESSAGES.app),
};
/** R3: only database imports mongodb/bson (static, type-only, re-export and dynamic import). */
const R3_MONGODB = {
  patterns: [{ regex: MONGODB_RE, message: MESSAGES.mongodb }],
  syntax: dynamicSelectors(MONGODB_RE, MESSAGES.mongodb),
};
/** R4: only config reads process.env (member access, destructuring, import, globalThis). */
const R4_ENV = {
  properties: [{ object: "process", property: "env", message: MESSAGES.env }],
  paths: [
    { name: "process", importNames: ["env"], message: MESSAGES.env },
    { name: "node:process", importNames: ["env"], message: MESSAGES.env },
  ],
  syntax: [
    {
      selector:
        "VariableDeclarator[init.name='process'] > ObjectPattern > Property[key.name='env']",
      message: MESSAGES.env,
    },
    {
      selector: "MemberExpression[object.property.name='process'][property.name='env']",
      message: MESSAGES.env,
    },
  ],
};

/**
 * Emits ONE config object with at most one entry each for no-restricted-imports,
 * no-restricted-syntax and no-restricted-properties, composed from the given atoms.
 */
function scope(files, ignores, atoms, { noConsole = false } = {}) {
  const paths = atoms.flatMap((atom) => atom.paths ?? []);
  const patterns = atoms.flatMap((atom) => atom.patterns ?? []);
  const syntax = atoms.flatMap((atom) => atom.syntax ?? []);
  const properties = atoms.flatMap((atom) => atom.properties ?? []);
  const rules = {};
  if (paths.length > 0 || patterns.length > 0) {
    const options = {};
    if (paths.length > 0) options.paths = paths;
    if (patterns.length > 0) options.patterns = patterns;
    rules["no-restricted-imports"] = ["error", options];
  }
  if (syntax.length > 0) rules["no-restricted-syntax"] = ["error", ...syntax];
  if (properties.length > 0) rules["no-restricted-properties"] = ["error", ...properties];
  if (noConsole) rules["no-console"] = "error";
  return ignores.length > 0 ? { files, ignores, rules } : { files, rules };
}

const SRC = { noConsole: true };
const NOT_DB_OR_CONFIG = ["src/modules/{database,config}/**"];
/** Files under src/modules already claimed by the module root / internal / nested-internal scopes. */
const MODULE_SCOPED_FILES = [
  "src/modules/*/*.{ts,tsx}",
  "src/modules/*/internal/*.{ts,tsx}",
  "src/modules/*/internal/*/**/*.{ts,tsx}",
];

const boundaryScopes = [
  // S1 app + other root src files (instrumentation has its own scope)
  scope(
    ["src/app/**/*.{ts,tsx}", "src/*.{ts,tsx}"],
    ["src/instrumentation.ts"],
    [R1_DEEP, R1_REL_INTO_MODULES, R3_MONGODB, R4_ENV],
    SRC,
  ),
  // S2 instrumentation: process.env allowed (Next runtime file)
  scope(["src/instrumentation.ts"], [], [R1_DEEP, R1_REL_INTO_MODULES, R3_MONGODB], SRC),
  // S0 catch-all for any other src file (src/lib, src/components, ...): base rules
  scope(
    ["src/**/*.{ts,tsx}"],
    ["src/app/**", "src/*.{ts,tsx}", "src/modules/**"],
    [R1_DEEP, R1_REL_INTO_MODULES, R3_MONGODB, R4_ENV],
    SRC,
  ),
  // S3/S4 database: mongodb allowed
  scope(["src/modules/database/*.{ts,tsx}"], [], [R1_DEEP, R1_ESCAPE_1, R2_APP, R4_ENV], SRC),
  scope(
    ["src/modules/database/internal/*.{ts,tsx}"],
    [],
    [R1_DEEP, R1_ESCAPE_2, R2_APP, R4_ENV],
    SRC,
  ),
  // S5/S6 config: process.env allowed
  scope(["src/modules/config/*.{ts,tsx}"], [], [R1_DEEP, R1_ESCAPE_1, R2_APP, R3_MONGODB], SRC),
  scope(
    ["src/modules/config/internal/*.{ts,tsx}"],
    [],
    [R1_DEEP, R1_ESCAPE_2, R2_APP, R3_MONGODB],
    SRC,
  ),
  // S7/S8 every other module. These globs already cover auth, wallet and security (and every
  // future module), so no per-module scope is added: a second scope for the same files would
  // REPLACE these rules (see PITFALL above), not add to them.
  scope(
    ["src/modules/*/*.{ts,tsx}"],
    NOT_DB_OR_CONFIG,
    [R1_DEEP, R1_ESCAPE_1, R2_APP, R3_MONGODB, R4_ENV],
    SRC,
  ),
  scope(
    ["src/modules/*/internal/*.{ts,tsx}"],
    NOT_DB_OR_CONFIG,
    [R1_DEEP, R1_ESCAPE_2, R2_APP, R3_MONGODB, R4_ENV],
    SRC,
  ),
  // S9 nested internal directories are forbidden outright (and still get the module rules)
  scope(
    ["src/modules/*/internal/*/**/*.{ts,tsx}"],
    NOT_DB_OR_CONFIG,
    [R1_FLAT_INTERNAL, R1_DEEP, R1_REL_INTO_MODULES, R2_APP, R3_MONGODB, R4_ENV],
    SRC,
  ),
  scope(
    ["src/modules/database/internal/*/**/*.{ts,tsx}"],
    [],
    [R1_FLAT_INTERNAL, R1_DEEP, R1_REL_INTO_MODULES, R2_APP, R4_ENV],
    SRC,
  ),
  scope(
    ["src/modules/config/internal/*/**/*.{ts,tsx}"],
    [],
    [R1_FLAT_INTERNAL, R1_DEEP, R1_REL_INTO_MODULES, R2_APP, R3_MONGODB],
    SRC,
  ),
  // S9b catch-all for any other file under src/modules (module/lib/x.ts, modules/x.ts, ...):
  // module rules; a depth-1 subdirectory may not climb into another module.
  scope(
    ["src/modules/**/*.{ts,tsx}"],
    [...MODULE_SCOPED_FILES, ...NOT_DB_OR_CONFIG],
    [R1_DEEP, R1_REL_INTO_MODULES, R1_ESCAPE_2, R2_APP, R3_MONGODB, R4_ENV],
    SRC,
  ),
  scope(
    ["src/modules/database/**/*.{ts,tsx}"],
    MODULE_SCOPED_FILES,
    [R1_DEEP, R1_REL_INTO_MODULES, R1_ESCAPE_2, R2_APP, R4_ENV],
    SRC,
  ),
  scope(
    ["src/modules/config/**/*.{ts,tsx}"],
    MODULE_SCOPED_FILES,
    [R1_DEEP, R1_REL_INTO_MODULES, R1_ESCAPE_2, R2_APP, R3_MONGODB],
    SRC,
  ),
  // S10 unit tests
  scope(["tests/unit/**/*.ts"], [], [R1_DEEP, R1_REL_INTO_MODULES, R3_MONGODB, R4_ENV]),
  // S11 integration tests: mongodb allowed (labelled MongoErrors, ClientSession spies)
  scope(["tests/integration/**/*.ts"], [], [R1_DEEP, R1_REL_INTO_MODULES, R4_ENV]),
  // S12 test setup and helpers: process.env allowed
  scope(["tests/setup/**/*.ts", "tests/helpers/**/*.ts"], [], [R1_DEEP, R1_REL_INTO_MODULES]),
];
// Root config files (*.config.ts, eslint.config.mjs) are deliberately unscoped: they are the
// spec's "Next/tooling config files" exception.

export default defineConfig([
  globalIgnores([
    ".next/**",
    "out/**",
    "build/**",
    "node_modules/**",
    "coverage/**",
    "next-env.d.ts",
    "tests/fixtures/**",
    ".agenkit/**",
    ".claude/**",
  ]),
  ...nextVitals,
  ...nextTs,
  unusedVars,
  ...boundaryScopes,
  prettier,
]);
```

Replace the whole of `README.md` (an existing file) with:

````markdown
# GHOST WIRE

**Your money. Your business. Your privacy.**

GHOST WIRE is a consumer-friendly, self-custodial private payments application for Solana. The initial product focuses on sending and receiving SOL and USDC using Helius Privacy Rings, with an embedded-wallet path and support for existing wallets such as Phantom and Solflare, subject to verified compatibility.

> **Project status:** Foundation built (PR 01: app skeleton, config, database layer, health/readiness routes, module boundaries); no wallet, privacy or payment features yet. This repository is the source of truth for product intent, design direction, architecture decisions, open questions, and implementation guidance. Documented concepts are not necessarily implemented.

## Start here

- [Product overview](docs/PRODUCT_OVERVIEW.md)
- [Brand identity](docs/BRAND_IDENTITY.md)
- [Frontend design](docs/FRONTEND_DESIGN.md)
- [Technical architecture](docs/TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md)
- [Data model](docs/DATA_MODEL.md)
- [User flows](docs/USER_FLOWS.md)
- [Security and privacy](docs/SECURITY_AND_PRIVACY.md)
- [Implementation roadmap](docs/IMPLEMENTATION_ROADMAP.md)
- [Agent guide](docs/AGENT_GUIDE.md)
- [Open decisions](docs/OPEN_DECISIONS.md)

## Product in one paragraph

GHOST WIRE makes supported private SOL and USDC payments approachable. Users can create an embedded wallet or connect an existing supported wallet, initialize privacy functionality, deposit assets into private balances, send confidential transfers, receive payments, and withdraw to public Solana balances. Payment links, QR requests, transaction history, and receipts make the experience useful for everyday users and freelancers/businesses without requiring them to understand the underlying cryptography.

## Locked decisions

| Area                   | Decision                                                                                          |
| ---------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Product name           | GHOST WIRE                                                                                        |
| Tagline                | Your money. Your business. Your privacy.                                                          |
| Audience               | Everyday crypto users and freelancers/businesses                                                  |
| Interface              | Consumer-friendly, suitable for business use                                                      |
| Visual direction       | Black/graphite, silver, electric violet                                                           |
| Chain                  | Solana                                                                                            |
| Initial assets         | SOL and USDC                                                                                      |
| Wallet experience      | Embedded plus existing-wallet connection (path unverified; see ADR-0005 risks)                    |
| Privacy infrastructure | Helius Privacy Rings                                                                              |
| MVP                    | Wallets, private balances, deposits, transfers, withdrawals; then links, QR, history and receipts |
| Token                  | Not required for MVP; revisit after product usage is validated                                    |

## Core principles

1. **Privacy by default, honestly described.** Explain what is encrypted, what remains visible, and where public/private boundaries occur.
2. **Self-custody.** Users authorize signing. Never collect seed phrases or put private keys in application databases.
3. **Simple experience.** Keep blockchain complexity behind clear payment flows.
4. **Correct payment state.** A payment is not complete merely because a client submitted a transaction or an API returned successfully.
5. **No invented integrations.** Verify Helius SDK APIs, wallet compatibility, asset support, and production readiness against current official documentation.
6. **Lean scope.** Prove reliable SOL and USDC transfers before expanding or introducing token mechanics.

## Important privacy limitation

Do not describe the default permissionless Privacy Ring as anonymous or untraceable. Confidential transfer details such as asset and amount may be encrypted, while sender and recipient addresses and transaction execution remain publicly observable. Deposits and withdrawals are privacy boundary crossings. Confirm exact behavior for each integration path before writing user-facing claims.

## Development rule

Start with repository discovery and a technical proof of concept. The first hard gate is two independently controlled wallets successfully depositing, sending, receiving, and withdrawing both SOL and USDC in the target environment, with correct transaction reconciliation and privacy behavior. Do not build the full payment-link experience on unverified transfer assumptions.

## Development setup

### Prerequisites

- **Node 24** (`.nvmrc`; `nvm use`). Node is the only runtime ([ADR-0002](docs/adr/0002-bun-tooling-node-runtime.md)).
- **Bun 1.4.4**, used only as the package manager and script runner.
- **MongoDB 7.0+ as a replica set** for local development (transactions need one). Tests start their own in-memory replica set.

### First run

```bash
bun install --frozen-lockfile
cp .env.example .env.local        # then edit values; never commit .env files
mkdir -p .data/db
mongod --replSet rs0 --dbpath ./.data/db --bind_ip 127.0.0.1 &
mongosh --quiet --eval "rs.initiate()"
bun run dev                       # http://localhost:3000, /api/health, /api/ready
```

The server validates configuration at boot and exits with code 1 if any key is missing or invalid.
The error names the keys, never their values. This applies to `bun run dev` as well as
`bun run start`: after a bad `.env`, fix it and restart the server.

### Configuration

| Key                                     | Required             | Description                                                                                                                                                                 |
| --------------------------------------- | -------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `MONGODB_URI`                           | yes                  | `mongodb://` or `mongodb+srv://` connection string (replica set for transactions)                                                                                           |
| `MONGODB_DB_NAME`                       | yes                  | 1–63 chars of `A-Z a-z 0-9 _ -`                                                                                                                                             |
| `LOG_LEVEL`                             | no                   | `debug`, `info` (default), `warn` or `error`                                                                                                                                |
| `APP_ORIGIN`                            | yes                  | Public origin, `https://host[:port]` or `http://host[:port]` (local dev), no path or trailing slash. `https` gives the `__Host-gw_session` cookie                           |
| `SOLANA_NETWORKS`                       | no                   | Comma list of `devnet` and/or `mainnet-beta` (default: both). A `mainnet-beta` wallet does not imply privacy readiness ([ADR-0006](docs/adr/0006-wallet-signature-auth.md)) |
| `TRUST_PROXY`                           | no                   | `true` only behind a proxy that sets `X-Forwarded-For`; default `false` (all clients share one rate-limit bucket)                                                           |
| `SESSION_IDLE_DAYS`                     | no                   | Session idle lifetime, integer 1–30 (default `7`)                                                                                                                           |
| `SESSION_MAX_DAYS`                      | no                   | Session absolute lifetime, integer ≥ `SESSION_IDLE_DAYS` and ≤ 90 (default `30`)                                                                                            |
| `RATE_LIMIT_CHALLENGE_PER_MIN`          | no                   | Challenges per client IP per minute, integer 1–1000 (default `10`)                                                                                                          |
| `RATE_LIMIT_CHALLENGE_PER_HOUR_ADDRESS` | no                   | Challenges per wallet address per hour, integer 1–1000 (default `20`)                                                                                                       |
| `RATE_LIMIT_VERIFY_PER_MIN`             | no                   | Verify attempts per client IP per minute, integer 1–1000 (default `10`)                                                                                                     |
| `NODE_ENV`                              | set by tooling       | `development`, `test` or `production`. Set by Next.js and Vitest                                                                                                            |
| `MONGOMS_SYSTEM_BINARY`                 | tests only, optional | Path to a local `mongod` 7.0.x (e.g. `/usr/bin/mongod`) instead of downloading one                                                                                          |

### Scripts

| Command                                          | What it does                                                        |
| ------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `bun run dev`                                    | Next.js dev server                                                  |
| `bun run build`                                  | Production build (works without a `.env`)                           |
| `bun run start`                                  | Serve the production build                                          |
| `bun run lint`                                   | ESLint, including the module boundary rules (zero warnings allowed) |
| `bun run typecheck`                              | `next typegen` then `tsc --noEmit`                                  |
| `bun run test`                                   | All Vitest projects (unit + integration)                            |
| `bun run test:unit` / `bun run test:integration` | One project only                                                    |
| `bun run test:coverage`                          | Tests with a V8 coverage report in `coverage/` (no threshold)       |
| `bun run format` / `bun run format:check`        | Prettier write / check                                              |

> **Never run `bun test`.** It starts Bun's built-in test runner, not Vitest. Always use `bun run test`.

On hosts without a downloadable MongoDB build (for example Ubuntu 20.04), export
`MONGOMS_SYSTEM_BINARY=/usr/bin/mongod` before running tests. An unrelated developer `mongod` may be
listening on localhost:27017; the tests never use that port.

**Known issue:** Bun can install `node_modules/prettier/bin/prettier.cjs` without the execute bit, so
`bun run format` and `bun run format:check` fail with exit 126 "Permission denied" on some hosts. Run
`node node_modules/prettier/bin/prettier.cjs --check .` (or `--write <files>`) instead.

### Auth API

Wallet-signature sign-in ([ADR-0006](docs/adr/0006-wallet-signature-auth.md)). Every response is
`Cache-Control: no-store` JSON; every non-GET request needs `Origin` equal to `APP_ORIGIN`.
"Session" means a valid session cookie.

| Method and path                       | Auth    | Success                                                |
| ------------------------------------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| `POST /api/v1/auth/challenge`         | none    | 200 `{ challengeId, message, expiresAt }`              |
| `POST /api/v1/auth/verify`            | none    | 200 `{ user, wallet }` and the session cookie          |
| `POST /api/v1/auth/logout`            | session | 204, revokes the session and clears the cookie         |
| `GET /api/v1/me`                      | session | 200 `{ user, session, wallets }`                       |
| `GET /api/v1/sessions`                | session | 200 `{ sessions }`                                     |
| `DELETE /api/v1/sessions/:id`         | session | 204 (own sessions only, otherwise 404)                 |
| `GET /api/v1/wallets`                 | session | 200 `{ wallets }`                                      |
| `POST /api/v1/wallets/link/challenge` | session | 200 `{ challengeId, message, expiresAt }`              |
| `POST /api/v1/wallets/link/verify`    | session | 201 `{ wallet }` (200 if the wallet was already yours) |
| `DELETE /api/v1/wallets/:id`          | session | 204; 409 `LAST_WALLET` for the only active wallet      |

Errors are `{ code, message }`: `VALIDATION_FAILED`, `NETWORK_UNSUPPORTED` (400), `AUTH_FAILED`,
`UNAUTHENTICATED` (401), `ORIGIN_REJECTED` (403), `NOT_FOUND` (404), `WALLET_ALREADY_LINKED`,
`LAST_WALLET` (409), `RATE_LIMITED` (429, with `Retry-After`), `DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` (503,
with `Retry-After`) and `INTERNAL` (500). Every response states the wallet or session `network`;
nothing in this API claims privacy capability.

### Module boundaries

Application code lives in `src/modules/<name>/`, each with a public `index.ts` and a flat `internal/`
directory ([ADR-0001](docs/adr/0001-single-app-module-boundaries.md)). `bun run lint` enforces:

1. Outside a module, import only `@/modules/<name>`. Deep imports (`@/modules/<name>/internal/...`) are errors.
2. Files under `src/modules/**` never import from `src/app/**`.
3. Only `src/modules/database/**` may import `mongodb`.
4. Only `src/modules/config/**` may read `process.env` (exceptions: tooling config files, `src/instrumentation.ts`, test setup).

`auth` depends only on `wallet`, `security`, `database`, `config` and `shared`. Only
`src/modules/wallet/internal/base58.ts` imports `bs58` (reviewed in code review, not linted).

The rules also cover dynamic `import()` and `require()`. The lint scopes are an allowlist of paths, so a
new top-level directory (such as `scripts/` or `tests/e2e/`) or new extensions (such as `.mts` or `.js`)
need their own scope in `eslint.config.mjs`.

All logging goes through the redacting logger in `@/modules/shared`; `console.*` is not allowed in `src/`.

### Decisions

Stack decisions are recorded in [`docs/adr/`](docs/adr/). Open questions live in
[`docs/OPEN_DECISIONS.md`](docs/OPEN_DECISIONS.md).
````

Create `docs/adr/0006-wallet-signature-auth.md` (Prettier-ignored, so copy it exactly):

````markdown
# ADR-0006: Self-hosted wallet-signature auth with server-side sessions

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Date:** 2026-11-13
- **Owner / approver:** Backend Architect (AgenKit) / project owner (approved in brainstorming, decisions A1–A5)

## Decision

GHOST WIRE authenticates users only by proof of Solana wallet ownership, with no email, password or
third-party auth vendor (A1). The backend API ships first as PR 02a; the branded connect UI follows
as PR 02b (A2).

- **Accounts (A3).** One account, many wallets. The first sign-in creates the account. A signed-in
  user can link more wallets, and any linked wallet signs in to the same account.
  `(address, network)` belongs to exactly one account (unique index
  `wallets_address_network_unique`). Unlinking the last active wallet is refused (`LAST_WALLET`).
- **Networks (A4).** `devnet` and `mainnet-beta` are both accepted from the start, through the
  `SOLANA_NETWORKS` allowlist (default: both).
- **Proof (A5).** The server builds a Sign-In-With-Solana message (CAIP-122 layout) and verifies
  the Ed25519 signature with `node:crypto`. No Wallet Standard `solana:signIn` helper and no JWT.
- **Sessions (A5).** Opaque 32-byte random tokens (base64url) in an HttpOnly cookie. Only
  `sha256(token)` is stored, in `sessions`.

### Message format (UTF-8, `\n` line endings, no trailing newline)

```
{domain} wants you to sign in with your Solana account:
{address}

Sign in to GHOST WIRE. This does not authorize any transaction or move funds.

URI: {origin}
Version: 1
Chain ID: solana:{network}
Nonce: {nonce}
Issued At: {issuedAt}
Expiration Time: {expiresAt}
```

`domain` is the host of `APP_ORIGIN` and `origin` is `APP_ORIGIN`. The nonce is 16 random bytes
as base64url. Both timestamps are ISO-8601 UTC with milliseconds; a challenge expires 5 minutes
after issue. Link challenges use the statement `Link this wallet to your GHOST WIRE account. This
does not authorize any transaction or move funds.` Only `sha256(nonce)` is stored as a field; the
nonce otherwise exists only inside the stored message. A challenge is consumed atomically by one
`findOneAndUpdate`, outside the sign-in transaction, so one challenge allows one signature attempt.

### Session model

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Cookie (https `APP_ORIGIN`) | `__Host-gw_session=<token>; Path=/; Max-Age=<s>; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Lax` |
| Cookie (http, local dev) | `gw_session=<token>; Path=/; Max-Age=<s>; HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax` |
| Idle lifetime | `SESSION_IDLE_DAYS` (default 7), sliding, written at most once per hour |
| Absolute lifetime | `SESSION_MAX_DAYS` (default 30), never extended |
| Revocation | `revokedAt` (logout, `DELETE /sessions/:id`, unlinking the wallet the session was created with) |
| CSRF | `Origin` must equal `APP_ORIGIN` on every non-GET request; `SameSite=Lax` is defence in depth |

### Mechanics decided in the architecture

- `bs58` 6.0.0 is the base58 decoder (approved 2026-11-13). Only
  `src/modules/wallet/internal/base58.ts` imports it in `src/`. PR 03 may swap it for
  `@solana/kit`'s codec behind the unchanged `wallet` API.
- Indexes are created by `prepareDatabase()` at boot (`src/instrumentation-node.ts`) and by the
  `ensureAuthReady()` request gate, both through the memoized `ensureIndexesOnce()`. An
  `INDEX_CONFLICT` at boot exits with code 1; an unreachable database at boot is deferred to the
  first request.
- Every variable filter value goes through `safeEquals`; server constants are written literally
  (approved 2026-11-13).
- The link routes use the same three rate limits as sign-in, under `link_*` keys (approved
  2026-11-13).
- The unlink transaction writes the owning `users` document first, so two concurrent unlinks
  write-conflict and the loser re-counts. Sign-in always writes the wallet document. Both guard
  writes use a value strictly later than the stored one, because MongoDB skips an update that sets
  a field to its current value, and a skipped update cannot conflict.

## Context

PR 02 of `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_ROADMAP.md` (authentication and wallet abstraction) was split into
02a and 02b. `docs/OPEN_DECISIONS.md` §2 asked for the auth provider, whether email is required and
whether accounts hold multiple wallets. ADR-0005 left Helius mainnet status unconfirmed.

## Rationale

- Wallet signatures are the product's identity primitive. Self-hosting keeps every credential
  decision, log line and stored field under this repository's redaction and review rules.
- Opaque, hashed, server-side sessions can be revoked one by one and leak nothing useful if the
  database is read. A stateless JWT cannot be revoked before it expires.
- `node:crypto` Ed25519 adds no dependency; the raw-key SPKI wrapper was verified against
  RFC 8032 §7.1 on Node 24.

## Alternatives considered

- **Hosted provider (Privy, Dynamic):** rejected (A1): third-party custody of identity and
  session data.
- **Email magic link plus wallet linking:** rejected (A1): adds email storage that the product
  does not need.
- **One wallet = one account; multi-wallet schema with linking deferred:** rejected (A3).
- **Devnet-only allowlist default:** rejected (A4).
- **Wallet Standard `solana:signIn` + `verifySignIn` helper; stateless JWT:** rejected (A5).
- **`@solana/kit` address codec or a hand-written base58 decoder:** rejected (architecture §1.2).

## Evidence / references

- Spec: `docs/specs/2026-11-13-auth-api-design.md`. Architecture:
  `docs/specs/2026-11-13-auth-api-architecture.md` (§7 records the three user decisions).
- Tests: `tests/unit/wallet/*.test.ts` (RFC 8032 vectors, message golden bytes, base58),
  `tests/integration/routes/v1/*.test.ts` (every spec §7 error row, the happy path, sessions,
  wallets, rate limits, logging, persistence), `tests/integration/auth/unlink-race.test.ts`.

## Consequences

- **Mainnet-binding risk (A4).** A `mainnet-beta` wallet can be bound before the Helius privacy
  layer is confirmed for mainnet (ADR-0005). Binding a mainnet wallet never implies privacy
  readiness. Every wallet and session response states its `network`, nothing in the API asserts
  privacy capability, and PR 02b must display the network prominently.
- With `TRUST_PROXY=false` behind a proxy, every client shares one rate-limit bucket. The deploy
  configuration must set `TRUST_PROXY` correctly.
- MongoDB-based rate limiting costs one write per auth request; revisit if load requires.
- `security_events` has no TTL until a retention policy is approved.
- Phantom and Solflare `signMessage` differences are checked manually in PR 02b.

## Scope / privacy / security impact

Never stored: private keys, seed phrases, raw session tokens, the nonce as a field, signatures, IP
addresses and email (asserted by `tests/integration/routes/v1/persistence.test.ts`). Logs never
contain the token, its hash, the nonce, the signature or the full message (asserted by
`tests/integration/routes/v1/logging.test.ts`). The specific `AUTH_FAILED` reason goes only to
`security_events.reason` and the redacting logger, never to the response. A comprehensive security
review of PR 02a is deferred to the Security Engineer.
````

Edit `docs/DATA_MODEL.md` (it is Prettier-ignored). Make these four replacements exactly.

1. Replace the whole `### users` section (from `### users` up to, not including, `### wallets`) with:

```markdown
### users

Implemented in PR 02a (owner: `auth`; schema `src/modules/auth/internal/collections.ts`).

- `_id`
- `status`: active | suspended | deleted (new users are active; no API changes it yet)
- `createdAt`, `updatedAt` (`updatedAt` is also the unlink write-skew guard, ADR-0006)

No email and no auth provider field: sign-in is wallet-signature only (ADR-0006). Preferences come later.

```

2. Replace the whole `### wallets` section (up to, not including, `### payment_requests`) with:

```markdown
### wallets

Implemented in PR 02a (owner: `wallet`; schema `src/modules/wallet/internal/collection.ts`).

- `_id`
- `userId`
- `address`: base58 Solana public key (decodes to exactly 32 bytes)
- `network`: devnet | mainnet-beta
- `walletType`: external (embedded wallets are out of scope until the provider is validated)
- `provider`: nullable informational label matching `^[a-z0-9_-]{1,32}$`, never trusted for behaviour
- `status`: active | disconnected
- `linkedAt` (reset when a disconnected wallet is linked again), `updatedAt`

One account, many wallets: `(address, network)` belongs to exactly one account, and the last active wallet cannot be unlinked. Privacy-initialization and capability fields arrive with the Helius adapter (PR 03). Binding a mainnet-beta wallet does not imply privacy readiness.

```

3. Replace the `### security_events` and `### sessions (if not handled by auth provider)` sections (from `### security_events` up to, not including, `## 3. Indexes`) with:

```markdown
### security_events

Implemented in PR 02a (owner: `security`; schema `src/modules/security/internal/events.ts`).

- `_id`
- `type`: signin_success | signin_failed | wallet_linked | link_failed | wallet_unlinked | session_revoked | rate_limited
- `severity`: info | warning | critical (`*_failed` and `rate_limited` are warning; nothing is critical yet)
- `userId` nullable, `walletId` nullable
- `reason`: nullable static string from a fixed list (never request data)
- `createdAt`

Written best effort after commit; never IPs, tokens, nonces, signatures or messages.

### sessions

Implemented in PR 02a (owner: `auth`). Opaque tokens; only `sha256(token)` is stored.

- `_id`, `tokenHash`, `userId`, `walletId` (the wallet used to sign in), `network`
- `createdAt`, `lastSeenAt`, `expiresAt` (idle, sliding at most hourly), `absoluteExpiresAt`
- `revokedAt` nullable, `userAgent` nullable (control characters stripped, at most 128 chars)

### auth_challenges

Implemented in PR 02a (owner: `auth`). One signature attempt per challenge.

- `_id`, `nonceHash` (`sha256(nonce)`), `message` (the exact signed text), `address`, `network`
- `purpose`: signin | link; `userId` (set for link, null for signin)
- `createdAt`, `expiresAt` (5 minutes), `consumedAt` nullable

### rate_limits

Implemented in PR 02a (owner: `security`). Fixed windows.

- `_id`, `key` (`sha256("<route>:<kind>:<subject>")`; the IP exists only inside this hash), `windowStart`, `count`, `expiresAt`

```

4. In `## 3. Indexes`, replace the `- wallets: wallet address + network; userId.` and `- security_events: userId + timestamp; TTL only under an approved retention policy.` bullets with:

```markdown
- wallets: `wallets_address_network_unique` (address + network, unique); `wallets_userId`.
- auth_challenges: `auth_challenges_nonceHash_unique` (unique); `auth_challenges_expiresAt_ttl` (TTL 3600 s after expiresAt).
- sessions: `sessions_tokenHash_unique` (unique); `sessions_userId`; `sessions_absoluteExpiresAt_ttl` (TTL 0 s).
- rate_limits: `rate_limits_key_windowStart_unique` (unique); `rate_limits_expiresAt_ttl` (TTL 0 s).
- security_events: `security_events_userId_createdAt` (userId + createdAt descending); no TTL until a retention policy is approved.
```

Then replace the line `Confirm uniqueness rules for multi-wallet and multi-network support.` with `Multi-wallet and multi-network uniqueness is decided in ADR-0006: one (address, network) per account. Indexes are created by \`prepareDatabase()\` at boot and the \`ensureAuthReady()\` request gate.`
